The neuron design achieves compact area utilization (around 540 NAND2-equivalent units) and low energy consumption (21.7 pJ/pulse) without requiring ADCs for information readout, making it ideal for system-on-chip integration of reservoir computing. This architecture paves the way for scalable, energy-efficient neuromorphic systems capable of performing real-time learning and inference with high configurability and digital interfacing.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a CMOS-implemented field-programmable spiking neural network (SNN) architecture designed for hardware reservoir computing (RC). The core innovation is a leaky integrate-and-fire (LIF) neuron circuit integrating voltage-controlled oscillators (VCOs) and a field-programmable gate array (FPGA) framework for weighted interconnections, which facilitates flexible reservoir configurations. The system is shown to implement the FORCE learning algorithm effectively, and benchmarks demonstrate linear/non-linear memory capacity and NARMA10 task performance. Key features include compact neuron area (around 540 NAND2 equivalents) and low energy consumption (21.7 pJ/pulse) without needing ADCs, making it suitable for system-on-chip integration in reservoir computing applications. This architecture promises scalable, energy-efficient neuromorphic systems enabling real-time learning and inference with high configurability and digital interfacing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **System Integration:** The integration of LIF neurons, VCOs, and an FPGA-based routing architecture on a single chip is a significant engineering accomplishment. It enables flexibility in reservoir topology and facilitates exploration of different network configurations.
    *   **Low Power:** The reported energy consumption (21.7 pJ/pulse) is attractive, particularly given the challenges of implementing analog computation in CMOS. This feature addresses a primary motivation for using SNNs in edge computing applications. The avoidance of ADCs is a key factor in reducing power and area overhead.
    *   **Benchmarking:** The paper provides experimental and simulation results on standard reservoir computing benchmarks, including linear/non-linear memory capacity and NARMA10. These benchmarks give a good indication of the system's functional capabilities.
    *   **Hardware Implementation:** A working chip with measurements is provided, which is always more convincing than simulations alone. The paper compares simulation results with actual chip measurements, providing insights into hardware limitations and discrepancies. The detail in the measurements is excellent.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and the architecture and methods are explained clearly.
    *   **FPGA Integration:** The utilization of an FPGA to dynamically configure the network is very valuable and enables the exploration of various network topologies/hyperparameters during runtime.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Novelty of Components:** While the *integration* is novel, the individual components (LIF neurons, VCOs, FPGAs) are well-established. The real innovation comes from their combination. The paper does not fully expand on the uniqueness of the proposed approach compared to other RC implementations using various technologies.
    *   **Scalability:** The paper implements 100 neurons. While this demonstrates feasibility, it's not a particularly large-scale system. The challenges of scaling this architecture to thousands or millions of neurons are not thoroughly addressed, but this is usually the case in such implementations.
    *   **Learning Algorithm Comparison:** The paper primarily focuses on the FORCE algorithm. A comparison to other reservoir computing training methods (e.g., standard linear regression) would provide a better understanding of the effectiveness of the combined architecture.
    *   **Reservoir Dynamics Analysis:** The dynamics of the reservoir are not analyzed in depth. Metrics like the echo state property or Lyapunov exponents would provide more quantitative insights into the reservoir's computational capabilities.
    *   **Noise Sensitivity:** While the paper mentions that the digital transmission of signals provides some noise immunity, it doesn't provide a thorough analysis of the system's robustness to noise. Analog implementations are susceptible to noise and variability, especially in advanced CMOS nodes, and detailed characterization is essential.
    *   **FORCE Hardware:** A thorough explanation of the hardware used for FORCE in the FPGA is still necessary to fully evaluate the system.
*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper presents a practically relevant solution for implementing reservoir computing in hardware. The low-power characteristics and FPGA-based reconfigurability are desirable for edge computing applications.
    *   The work offers insights into the design trade-offs involved in implementing SNNs in CMOS, particularly regarding area, power, and configurability.
    *   This type of implementation allows better characterization of SNNs.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a well-engineered system with valuable practical implications. The combination of analog neurons with digital routing offers a balance between energy efficiency and configurability. However, the analysis of reservoir dynamics and noise robustness could be more in-depth. More detailed comparisons of various topologies would be beneficial as well.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper represents a solid, incremental advancement in the field of hardware neuromorphic computing. It's not a groundbreaking theoretical breakthrough, but it showcases a practical, well-documented implementation with relevant performance metrics. The strength lies in the system integration, low-power design, and experimental validation. The weaknesses are the limited scalability demonstrated, the lack of in-depth dynamical analysis, and the need for more elaborate benchmarks and comparisons to other architectures. The paper makes a real contribution by realizing reservoir computing in hardware that can be further built upon, so the score has been adjusted accordingly. It is a good, solid engineering contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.NE
- **Score**: 7/10

### MLLM-Driven Semantic Identifier Generation for Generative Cross-Modal Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17359v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyuan Li, Lei Wang, Ahtamjan Ahmat, Yating Yang, Bo Ma, Rui Dong, Bangju Han
- **Abstract**: Generative cross-modal retrieval, which treats retrieval as a generation task, has emerged as a promising direction with the rise of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). In this setting, the model responds to a text query by generating an identifier corresponding to the target image. However, existing methods typically rely on manually crafted string IDs, clustering-based labels, or atomic identifiers requiring vocabulary expansion, all of which face challenges in semantic alignment or scalability.To address these limitations, we propose a vocabulary-efficient identifier generation framework that prompts MLLMs to generate Structured Semantic Identifiers from image-caption pairs. These identifiers are composed of concept-level tokens such as objects and actions, naturally aligning with the model's generation space without modifying the tokenizer. Additionally, we introduce a Rationale-Guided Supervision Strategy, prompting the model to produce a one-sentence explanation alongside each identifier serves as an auxiliary supervision signal that improves semantic grounding and reduces hallucinations during training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for generative cross-modal retrieval using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The core idea is to train MLLMs to generate structured semantic identifiers (SSIDs) from image-caption pairs. These identifiers are composed of concept-level tokens (e.g., objects, actions) extracted directly by the MLLM, aligning with the model's generative distribution and avoiding the need for vocabulary expansion.  A "Rationale-Guided Supervision Strategy" is also introduced, where the model produces a one-sentence explanation alongside each identifier to improve semantic grounding and reduce hallucinations. The approach is evaluated on MSCOCO and Flickr30K datasets, demonstrating improvements over handcrafted and clustering-based identifiers while maintaining vocabulary efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using MLLMs to *generate* structured semantic identifiers, rather than relying on manual construction or clustering, is reasonably novel. Existing generative retrieval methods typically focus on text retrieval or simpler identifier schemes. The Rationale-Guided Supervision Strategy is a helpful addition that leverages the MLLM's language generation capabilities to improve the quality of the identifiers.  The combination of these elements constitutes a contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in generative cross-modal retrieval: how to design effective identifiers that are both semantically rich and scalable for large MLLMs. The SSID approach offers a compelling alternative to vocabulary expansion, which can become computationally expensive and limit the applicability of generative retrieval.  The improvements demonstrated on standard datasets suggest the potential of this approach to advance the state-of-the-art in cross-modal retrieval.  The claim of being vocabulary efficient is a major selling point, making the method more practical.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing identifier design strategies in the context of MLLMs.
    * **Well-Defined Approach:**  The SSID framework and the Rationale-Guided Supervision Strategy are clearly explained and well-motivated.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvements over relevant baselines on standard datasets. The ablation study provides insights into the contribution of the rationale component.
    * **Vocabulary Efficiency:** A major strength of the method is that it doesn't require vocabulary expansion.
    * **Generalizability:** Experiments show the method's ability to generalize across different MLLMs and into multilingual scenarios to a limited degree.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GRACE Framework:** The tight coupling with the GRACE framework and its specific supervision and decoding strategies limits the generalizability and flexibility of the method. It isn't clear how well it would work outside of this framework.
    * **Limited Evaluation Against Non-Generative Methods:**  The evaluation primarily focuses on comparisons with identifier design strategies within the GRACE framework. A more comprehensive evaluation against widely used *non-generative* retrieval systems (e.g., CLIP, BLIP) would provide a stronger assessment of its overall performance. This is acknowledged by the authors.
    * **Lack of Formal Identifier Evaluation:** The paper doesn't include a quantitative analysis of the identifiers themselves (e.g., semantic consistency, discriminative capability), relying solely on the end-to-end retrieval performance.  Direct evaluation of the identifier's quality would strengthen the analysis.
    * **Multilingual Generalization Limitations:** The zero-shot multilingual results show a significant performance drop for languages linguistically distant from English, indicating a need for further work in this area.
    * **Case Study Limitations:** The case studies are somewhat subjective.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in generative cross-modal retrieval by highlighting the importance of semantic identifier design and providing a practical, vocabulary-efficient approach. The Rationale-Guided Supervision Strategy could also be adopted in other generative tasks.  However, its reliance on the GRACE framework will likely limit its immediate adoption by the broader community.

**Overall:**  The paper presents a well-defined and empirically validated approach to structured semantic identifier generation for generative cross-modal retrieval. It addresses an important challenge and offers a practical solution.  While the reliance on the GRACE framework and the limited comparison with non-generative methods weaken its overall impact, the novelty and potential of the approach justify a reasonably strong rating.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Pre-Trained CNN Architecture for Transformer-Based Image Caption Generation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17365v1)
- **Authors**: Amanuel Tafese Dufera
- **Abstract**: Automatic image captioning, a multifaceted task bridging computer vision and natural lan- guage processing, aims to generate descriptive textual content from visual input. While Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) networks have achieved significant advancements, they present limitations. The inherent sequential nature of RNNs leads to sluggish training and inference times. LSTMs further struggle with retaining information from earlier sequence elements when dealing with very long se- quences. This project presents a comprehensive guide to constructing and comprehending transformer models for image captioning. Transformers employ self-attention mechanisms, capturing both short- and long-range dependencies within the data. This facilitates efficient parallelization during both training and inference phases. We leverage the well-established Transformer architecture, recognized for its effectiveness in managing sequential data, and present a meticulous methodology. Utilizing the Flickr30k dataset, we conduct data pre- processing, construct a model architecture that integrates an EfficientNetB0 CNN for fea- ture extraction, and train the model with attention mechanisms incorporated. Our approach exemplifies the utilization of parallelization for efficient training and inference. You can find the project on GitHub.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of a Transformer-based model for image captioning, focusing on its advantages over traditional CNN-RNN approaches. It highlights the Transformer architecture's ability to parallelize computations and capture long-range dependencies. The authors describe a methodology involving data preprocessing using the Flickr30k dataset, using a pre-trained EfficientNetB0 CNN for feature extraction, and training a Transformer with attention mechanisms. The paper presents results based on BLEU scores, demonstrating the model's ability to generate grammatically valid descriptions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper lacks significant novelty. Transformer-based architectures for image captioning are already well-established in the field. The use of a pre-trained EfficientNetB0 is a reasonable choice, but not a particularly innovative one. The methods of data preparation are standard practice in machine learning. The paper presents no new algorithms or architectural insights.
*   **Significance:** While the paper demonstrates a working implementation of a Transformer-based image captioning system, it does not offer substantial contributions to the field. Existing methods, particularly those that utilize transformer networks, already surpass the findings provided. The dataset used (Flickr30k) is also commonplace, and the BLEU scores, though acceptable, don't reflect state-of-the-art performance. The paper doesn't address any specific challenges or propose innovative solutions.
*   **Strengths:** The paper clearly outlines the standard methodology and provides a concise description of the model architecture. The qualitative results show examples of reasonably good captions generated by the model, and includes information about implementation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of Innovation:** The core concepts are well-known, and the application is straightforward.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study is limited to a single, relatively small dataset, making it difficult to generalize the findings.
    *   **Missing Comparisons:** The paper lacks a strong comparative analysis against other recent and relevant methods.  Simply stating that transformers outperform RNNs isn't sufficient; it should include a comparison against the best currently available solutions.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation primarily relies on BLEU scores, which are known to have limitations. Other metrics could have provided a more comprehensive assessment. The paper mentions parameter optimization via experimentation, but does not describe it.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper essentially replicates existing techniques, applying them to a known problem. While the implementation is functional, it doesn't break new ground or offer significant performance improvements over existing state-of-the-art techniques. The lack of innovation and the limited scope of the evaluation significantly reduce the paper's impact. Its value primarily lies in its expository explanation of existing technology.

Score: 4

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 4/10

### SilentStriker:Toward Stealthy Bit-Flip Attacks on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17371v1)
- **Authors**: Haotian Xu, Qingsong Peng, Jie Shi, Huadi Zheng, Yu Li, Cheng Zhuo
- **Abstract**: The rapid adoption of large language models (LLMs) in critical domains has spurred extensive research into their security issues. While input manipulation attacks (e.g., prompt injection) have been well studied, Bit-Flip Attacks (BFAs) -- which exploit hardware vulnerabilities to corrupt model parameters and cause severe performance degradation -- have received far less attention. Existing BFA methods suffer from key limitations: they fail to balance performance degradation and output naturalness, making them prone to discovery. In this paper, we introduce SilentStriker, the first stealthy bit-flip attack against LLMs that effectively degrades task performance while maintaining output naturalness. Our core contribution lies in addressing the challenge of designing effective loss functions for LLMs with variable output length and the vast output space. Unlike prior approaches that rely on output perplexity for attack loss formulation, which inevitably degrade output naturalness, we reformulate the attack objective by leveraging key output tokens as targets for suppression, enabling effective joint optimization of attack effectiveness and stealthiness. Additionally, we employ an iterative, progressive search strategy to maximize attack efficacy. Experiments show that SilentStriker significantly outperforms existing baselines, achieving successful attacks without compromising the naturalness of generated text.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SilentStriker: Toward Stealthy Bit-Flip Attacks on Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SilentStriker, a novel bit-flip attack (BFA) targeting Large Language Models (LLMs). Unlike existing BFA methods that either have limited impact or produce easily detectable gibberish outputs, SilentStriker aims for stealth by degrading LLM performance while maintaining the naturalness of the generated text.  The core contribution is a token-based loss function that suppresses the probability of critical "key tokens" appearing in the output, thereby guiding the model to generate incorrect answers without drastically affecting the overall fluency.  The method also employs an iterative search strategy to identify vulnerable bits and incorporates perplexity loss to maintain output coherence. Experiments on various LLMs and tasks demonstrate SilentStriker's superior performance in degrading accuracy while preserving naturalness compared to existing BFA baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its emphasis on *stealth* in a BFA attack against LLMs.  Prior work has focused on either causing catastrophic failure or bypassing safety mechanisms, but has paid less attention to generating outputs that are *plausible* yet incorrect.  The introduction of the token-based loss function, specifically targeting key tokens, is a significant innovation.  The iterative search strategy, while not entirely novel in itself (as acknowledged), is adapted and applied effectively in this context. The paper's approach to balancing performance degradation and output naturalness is a unique contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work stems from its demonstration that LLMs are vulnerable to subtle, yet effective, hardware-level attacks that are difficult to detect.  This has significant implications for the deployment of LLMs in critical applications where reliability and trustworthiness are paramount.  The paper highlights the fact that simply monitoring perplexity isn't sufficient to detect all forms of adversarial attacks against LLMs. By showcasing this vulnerability, the paper motivates the need for more robust defensive strategies that go beyond simple output monitoring and hardware security mechanisms.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of stealthy attacks on LLMs and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Technically sound:**  The proposed method is well-explained and technically sound. The token-based loss function is a clever way to guide the attack without completely destroying the output's coherence.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experimental results on multiple LLMs and tasks, demonstrating the effectiveness of SilentStriker. The comparison against existing baselines is thorough and convincing. The use of both accuracy metrics and GPT-based naturalness scores provides a comprehensive evaluation. The hyperparameter ablation studies further strengthens the claims.
    *   **Realistic threat model:** The threat model considered, involving edge devices with limited hardware security, is relevant and realistic.
    *   **Good writing quality:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited defense discussion:** While the paper mentions the lack of effective defenses, it doesn't explore potential defense mechanisms in detail.  More discussion on potential countermeasures would strengthen the paper's overall impact.
    *   **Generalizability to MoE models:** The limitations section mentions that the evaluation does not include Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models. Given the increasing prevalence of MoE models, this is a notable limitation.

*   **Potential influence on the field:** This paper will likely influence future research on adversarial attacks against LLMs. It provides a new perspective on BFA by emphasizing stealth and presents a practical method for achieving it. The token-based loss function could inspire other adversarial techniques that focus on manipulating specific aspects of the output without sacrificing overall quality.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a novel and technically sound method for stealthy bit-flip attacks against LLMs. Its emphasis on maintaining output naturalness is a valuable addition to the field of LLM security. The comprehensive evaluation demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While the lack of a detailed defense discussion and its applicability to MOE models constitutes a minor weakness, the overall novelty, significance, and technical rigor of the paper justify a high score. It will likely impact future research in this area and highlight the need for more robust defense mechanisms.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Robustness of Neurosymbolic Reasoners on First-Order Logic Problems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17377v1)
- **Authors**: Hannah Bansal, Kemal Kurniawan, Lea Frermann
- **Abstract**: Recent trends in NLP aim to improve reasoning capabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs), with key focus on generalization and robustness to variations in tasks. Counterfactual task variants introduce minimal but semantically meaningful changes to otherwise valid first-order logic (FOL) problem instances altering a single predicate or swapping roles of constants to probe whether a reasoning system can maintain logical consistency under perturbation. Previous studies showed that LLMs becomes brittle on counterfactual variations, suggesting that they often rely on spurious surface patterns to generate responses. In this work, we explore if a neurosymbolic (NS) approach that integrates an LLM and a symbolic logical solver could mitigate this problem. Experiments across LLMs of varying sizes show that NS methods are more robust but perform worse overall that purely neural methods. We then propose NSCoT that combines an NS method and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting and demonstrate that while it improves performance, NSCoT still lags behind standard CoT. Our analysis opens research directions for future work.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the robustness of neurosymbolic (NS) methods to counterfactual perturbations in first-order logic (FOL) reasoning tasks. Counterfactual perturbations involve making minimal but semantically meaningful changes to FOL problem instances (e.g., changing "bird" to "mammal"). The authors compare a neurosymbolic approach (LINC) that combines a Large Language Model (LLM) with a symbolic FOL solver against purely neural LLM-based methods. They find that NS methods are more robust (i.e., less sensitive to counterfactual changes) but perform worse overall. To improve performance, they propose NSCoT, which integrates Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting with the NS approach. While NSCoT improves performance over LINC, it still lags behind standard CoT prompting. The authors conclude that NS methods offer robustness benefits, and NSCoT is a step towards improving their accuracy, but further research is needed.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its exploration of the robustness of NS methods on counterfactual FOL reasoning tasks. While counterfactual perturbations have been previously used to assess LLM robustness in other contexts, their application to NS methods in FOL reasoning is a worthwhile contribution. The introduction of NSCoT as a way to improve NS performance is also reasonably novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important issue in LLM research: their tendency to rely on spurious correlations and surface patterns rather than true reasoning. The demonstration that NS methods are more robust to counterfactual variations is significant because it suggests a potential pathway for developing more reliable and trustworthy AI systems. However, the lower overall performance of NS methods limits the immediate practical impact. NSCoT's improvement is a useful proof-of-concept.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Rigorous experimental design: The paper uses a well-defined set of counterfactual perturbations and compares multiple LLMs and methods.
    *   Clear and well-motivated research question: The paper focuses on a specific and relevant issue in LLM research.
    *   Thorough analysis: The paper provides a detailed analysis of the results, including error analysis and a discussion of the limitations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Dataset Size: The "RR" dataset used to evaluate robustness is relatively small (n=81).  Although the FOLIO validation set is larger, it lacks counterfactual examples, so the core finding about robustness is based on a relatively small dataset.
    *   Performance Gap: NSCoT still lags behind the simple CoT approach, reducing its practical utility. The study could be improved by exploring different methods for prompting the LLM, exploring different solvers, or examining the causes for the lower overall performance of NS Methods..
    *   ChatGPT Generated COT Rationale in NSCOT: Using ChatGPT generated rationales for NSCoT weakens the overall design of the paper. A better design would use human-generated rationales to match the COT approach.
    *   Limited scope of reasoning tasks: The experiments are limited to FOL reasoning.

**Overall Impression:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by highlighting the robustness benefits of NS methods. However, the performance gap between NS and purely neural methods needs to be addressed. Furthermore, the analysis would be strengthened by further improvement to the NSCOT approach to bring closer to the performance of COT and a larger dataset size of the counterfactual reasoning dataset used for NS models. This paper is a solid start to an important line of research but needs further improvement before claiming high significance in the field.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper makes a novel contribution by investigating NS methods on counterfactual FOL tasks and demonstrates their robustness. However, its impact is limited by the lower overall performance of NS methods compared to neural approaches and the size of the dataset used for robust reasoning. While NSCoT is a promising direction, it still has some weaknesses as COT outperforms it. The code is made publicly available which helps reproducibility and follow-on research. Further improvements that bring NS methods closer to the performance of COT for the general reasoning task will significantly increase the paper's value.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### EpiCache: Episodic KV Cache Management for Long Conversational Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17396v1)
- **Authors**: Minsoo Kim, Arnav Kundu, Han-Byul Kim, Richa Dixit, Minsik Cho
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have extended context lengths, enabling assistants to sustain long histories for coherent, personalized responses. This ability, however, hinges on Key-Value (KV) caching, whose memory grows linearly with dialogue length and quickly dominates under strict resource constraints. An active line of research for reducing this overhead is KV cache compression, which seeks to limit cache size while preserving accuracy. Yet existing methods face two major limitations: (i) evicting entries after full-context prefill causes unbounded peak memory, and (ii) query-dependent eviction narrows the cache to a single query, leading to degraded accuracy in multi-turn conversations. We introduce EpiCache, a training-free KV cache management framework for long conversational question answering (LongConvQA) under fixed memory budgets. EpiCache bounds cache growth through block-wise prefill and preserves topic-relevant context via episodic KV compression, which clusters conversation history into coherent episodes and applies episode-specific KV cache eviction. We further design an adaptive layer-wise budget allocation strategy that measures each layer's sensitivity to eviction and distributes the memory budget across layers accordingly. Across three LongConvQA benchmarks, EpiCache improves accuracy by up to 40% over recent baselines, sustains near-full KV accuracy under 4-6x compression, and reduces latency and memory by up to 2.4x and 3.5x, thereby enabling efficient multi-turn interaction under strict resource constraints.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and novelty assessment of the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EPICACHE, a training-free KV cache management framework designed for long conversational question answering (LongConvQA) tasks under strict memory budgets. It addresses two limitations of existing KV cache compression methods: unbounded peak memory usage caused by post-prefill compression, and degraded accuracy in multi-turn conversations due to query-dependent eviction. EPICACHE employs a three-stage approach: (1) clustering conversation history into coherent episodes, (2) performing block-wise prefill with episode-specific KV cache compression, and (3) matching incoming queries to the most relevant episode for answer generation. Additionally, EPICACHE implements an adaptive layer-wise budget allocation strategy to distribute memory based on each layer's sensitivity to eviction. The experimental results demonstrate that EPICACHE significantly improves accuracy and reduces latency and memory usage compared to existing baselines on LongConvQA benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a relevant problem:** KV cache management is a crucial challenge for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments, especially for long conversational tasks.
    *   **Novel framework:** The proposed EPICACHE framework introduces several innovative components like episodic clustering, episodic KV compression, and adaptive layer-wise budget allocation, which address the limitations of previous work effectively.
    *   **Training-free approach:** EPICACHE doesn't require training, making it more accessible and easier to implement compared to training-based compression methods.
    *   **Significant performance improvements:** Experimental results demonstrate substantial accuracy gains (up to 40%) and latency/memory reductions compared to strong baselines on multiple LongConvQA benchmarks.
    *   **Thorough evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation, including ablation studies, memory scalability analysis, and efficiency analysis, which supports the effectiveness of EPICACHE.
    *   The approach of bounding memory with block-wise prefill directly addresses the memory limitations when deploying long context LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The three-stage framework and layer-wise allocation increase the overall complexity of the system, which can make it harder to implement and fine-tune.
    *   **Clustering dependency:** The performance of EPICACHE relies heavily on the quality of the conversation clustering. While the paper uses a standard K-means clustering algorithm, it does not explore the impacts of different clustering techniques on LongConvQA performance.
    *   **Limited generalizability:** Although the method is training-free, the performance depends on empirically tuning the hyper-parameters such as the sharpness hyper-parameter 'a'.
    *   **Overhead costs:** The query embedding, episode matching, and KV cache retrieval introduce overheads in the process. While the paper claims the overheads are minimal (around 5% of per-turn latency), a detailed analysis of these costs is needed.
    *   **Medoid approach:** Represents the episode by only a medoid segment which reduces diversity.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The combination of episodic clustering, episodic KV compression with a patched prompt, and adaptive layer-wise budget allocation constitutes a novel approach to KV cache management.
    *   **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution by enabling efficient multi-turn interactions under strict resource constraints. The proposed EPICACHE framework offers a practical solution for deploying LLMs in real-world conversational AI applications with limited memory budgets.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some limitations, the combination of novel techniques and significant experimental results make it an important contribution to the field.  The approach is practical, demonstrating a considerable improvement in performance when deploying long context LLMs with restricted memory. The episodic approach also seems to be novel and significantly contributes to the KV cache compression methods. Specifically, the paper takes into consideration the episodic structure of the conversation which other existing compression methods do not account for.

Score: 8.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Diff-GNSS: Diffusion-based Pseudorange Error Estimation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17397v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaqi Zhu, Shouyi Lu, Ziyao Li, Guirong Zhuo, Lu Xiong
- **Abstract**: Global Navigation Satellite Systems (GNSS) are vital for reliable urban positioning. However, multipath and non-line-of-sight reception often introduce large measurement errors that degrade accuracy. Learning-based methods for predicting and compensating pseudorange errors have gained traction, but their performance is limited by complex error distributions. To address this challenge, we propose Diff-GNSS, a coarse-to-fine GNSS measurement (pseudorange) error estimation framework that leverages a conditional diffusion model to capture such complex distributions. Firstly, a Mamba-based module performs coarse estimation to provide an initial prediction with appropriate scale and trend. Then, a conditional denoising diffusion layer refines the estimate, enabling fine-grained modeling of pseudorange errors. To suppress uncontrolled generative diversity and achieve controllable synthesis, three key features related to GNSS measurement quality are used as conditions to precisely guide the reverse denoising process. We further incorporate per-satellite uncertainty modeling within the diffusion stage to assess the reliability of the predicted errors. We have collected and publicly released a real-world dataset covering various scenes. Experiments on public and self-collected datasets show that DiffGNSS consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines across multiple metrics. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first application of diffusion models to pseudorange error estimation. The proposed diffusion-based refinement module is plug-and-play and can be readily integrated into existing networks to markedly improve estimation accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Diff-GNSS, a novel coarse-to-fine framework for estimating pseudorange errors in GNSS measurements, particularly in challenging urban environments. It leverages a conditional diffusion model to capture the complex distributions of pseudorange errors. The framework consists of a Mamba-based module for coarse estimation and a conditional denoising diffusion layer for refinement. To improve the controllability of the generative process and increase accuracy, the diffusion process is conditioned on GNSS measurement quality features and per-satellite uncertainty. The authors collected and made publicly available a new real-world dataset for evaluation. Experiments on both public and self-collected datasets demonstrate that Diff-GNSS outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. The diffusion-based refinement module is designed to be plug-and-play, facilitating integration into existing networks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of diffusion models to the domain of pseudorange error estimation in GNSS is novel. While diffusion models have seen success in other fields like image synthesis and scene flow estimation, this is, according to the authors, the first attempt to apply them to this specific problem. The coarse-to-fine approach combined with the conditional diffusion model and the introduction of per-satellite uncertainty estimation adds further novelty.

*   **Significance:** Improving GNSS positioning accuracy in urban environments is crucial for autonomous vehicles, unmanned aerial vehicles, and other location-based services. The paper addresses a significant challenge by providing a method that effectively mitigates the impact of multipath and NLOS reception. The plug-and-play nature of the refinement module is particularly significant because it enables easy integration with existing GNSS processing pipelines.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Technical Contribution:** The combination of Mamba and diffusion models is a sound approach for tackling the complex distribution of pseudorange errors.
    *   **Effective Conditioning:** The use of GNSS measurement quality features and coarse estimation as conditions for the diffusion process helps to control the generative process and improve accuracy. The uncertainty estimation is a particularly interesting addition, allowing the system to be aware of its own limitations.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted on both public and self-collected datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of Diff-GNSS in different scenarios. The comparisons against SOTA baselines are convincing.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The creation and public release of a new real-world GNSS dataset are valuable contributions to the community.
    *   **Plug-and-play module:** The possibility to be integrated easily to other GNSS positioning modules.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Complexity:** While the paper mentions a runtime analysis and claims the method is suitable for real-time processing, a more detailed analysis of the computational cost, especially regarding the training of the diffusion model, would be valuable. Comparing it with a more efficient method like traditional Kalman filtering could also be useful.
    *   **Ablation depth:** Additional details about the results of each ablation could be reported.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in GNSS positioning by demonstrating the effectiveness of diffusion models in addressing the challenge of pseudorange error estimation. The plug-and-play nature of the proposed refinement module could lead to its adoption in existing GNSS processing systems. The publicly released dataset will also facilitate further research in this area.
*   **Room for improvement:** The method can be integrated in a full GNSS positioning pipeline. Further research can explore tight integration with factor-graph frameworks to develop an end-to-end pseudorange-based positioning system.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

Diff-GNSS presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of GNSS positioning by applying diffusion models to pseudorange error estimation. The combination of a coarse estimation module, conditional diffusion refinement, and uncertainty estimation is technically sound and well-evaluated. The publicly released dataset further enhances the value of the paper. While the computational complexity could be explored in more detail and more ablation results could be reported, the overall quality and potential impact of the paper justify a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DIWALI - Diversity and Inclusivity aWare cuLture specific Items for India: Dataset and Assessment of LLMs for Cultural Text Adaptation in Indian Context
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17399v1)
- **Authors**: Pramit Sahoo, Maharaj Brahma, Maunendra Sankar Desarkar
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are widely used in various tasks and applications. However, despite their wide capabilities, they are shown to lack cultural alignment \citep{ryan-etal-2024-unintended, alkhamissi-etal-2024-investigating} and produce biased generations \cite{naous-etal-2024-beer} due to a lack of cultural knowledge and competence. Evaluation of LLMs for cultural awareness and alignment is particularly challenging due to the lack of proper evaluation metrics and unavailability of culturally grounded datasets representing the vast complexity of cultures at the regional and sub-regional levels. Existing datasets for culture specific items (CSIs) focus primarily on concepts at the regional level and may contain false positives. To address this issue, we introduce a novel CSI dataset for Indian culture, belonging to 17 cultural facets. The dataset comprises $\sim$8k cultural concepts from 36 sub-regions. To measure the cultural competence of LLMs on a cultural text adaptation task, we evaluate the adaptations using the CSIs created, LLM as Judge, and human evaluations from diverse socio-demographic region. Furthermore, we perform quantitative analysis demonstrating selective sub-regional coverage and surface-level adaptations across all considered LLMs. Our dataset is available here: \href{https://huggingface.co/datasets/nlip/DIWALI}{https://huggingface.co/datasets/nlip/DIWALI}, project webpage\footnote{\href{https://nlip-lab.github.io/nlip/publications/diwali/}{https://nlip-lab.github.io/nlip/publications/diwali/}}, and our codebase with model outputs can be found here: \href{https://github.com/pramitsahoo/culture-evaluation}{https://github.com/pramitsahoo/culture-evaluation}.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "DIWALI - Diversity and Inclusivity aWare cuLture specific Items for India":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DIWALI, a novel, large-scale dataset of Culture Specific Items (CSIs) for Indian culture. The dataset covers 36 sub-regions of India and comprises ~8k cultural concepts across 17 cultural facets. The authors evaluate the cultural competence of various open-weight LLMs on a cultural text adaptation task, adapting text from American to Indian cultural contexts.  They employ a combination of automatic CSI-based evaluation, LLM-as-judge assessment, and human evaluation to analyze the adaptation quality, focusing on sub-regional coverage and the depth of adaptations. The study reveals that LLMs often perform surface-level adaptations and struggle to capture the deep cultural nuances required for emotionally resonant and contextually appropriate text generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of the DIWALI dataset is a significant contribution. Existing CSI datasets are often limited in scope, particularly regarding sub-regional diversity within India. DIWALI addresses this gap by providing a more granular and comprehensive resource. The multi-faceted evaluation framework, incorporating CSI-based scoring, LLM-as-judge, and human evaluation, is also novel in its approach to assessing cultural adaptation.

*   **Significance:**  The findings have important implications for the development and evaluation of LLMs. The paper highlights the limitations of current models in capturing cultural nuances, even with large-scale training data. This underscores the need for:

    *   More culturally sensitive training data.
    *   Improved evaluation metrics that go beyond surface-level changes.
    *   Development of techniques that allow LLMs to perform deeper, contextually relevant cultural adaptations. The paper's findings on sub-regional biases also point to the need for more balanced training data reflecting India's diverse cultures.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The DIWALI dataset is a valuable resource for researchers working on cultural text adaptation and LLM evaluation.
    *   **Multi-faceted Evaluation:** Combining automatic and human evaluation provides a more holistic and nuanced assessment of LLM performance.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper offers a thorough analysis of LLM adaptations, identifying weaknesses in sub-regional coverage and the depth of cultural understanding.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper provides a clear definition of cultural text adaptation and proposes a systematic approach for evaluating LLMs on this task.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of LLMs:** The study focuses on a specific set of open-weight LLMs, so the generalizability of the findings to other models (e.g., proprietary models like GPT-4) is uncertain.  While the choice of models is practical and useful for comparison, expanding the range of models would improve the robustness of the conclusions.
    *   **Potential for Bias in Human Evaluation:**  Although the authors considered diverse locations for human evaluators, five evaluators may not be sufficiently representative of India's large and heterogeneous population. The cultural relevance may vary substantially within the sub-regions themselves, presenting bias.
    *   **LLM as Judge Reliability:** The paper observed that LLM as Judge scores significantly inflated human scores which calls into question its reliability, requiring additional investigation.
    *   **Subjectivity in defining "deeper" adaptation:** Although the authors give examples of "shallow" and "deep" adaptation, the degree to which adaptation meets deep-level criteria could be somewhat subjective.

*   **Potential Influence:** The DIWALI dataset and evaluation framework will likely become a benchmark for future research on cultural text adaptation, LLM evaluation, and cross-cultural NLP. The paper's findings will also inform the development of new training methods and model architectures that are more culturally sensitive.

**Overall Score:**

Taking into account the strengths and weaknesses, as well as the novelty and significance of the work, I assign the paper a score of **7.5**. The creation and evaluation of the DIWALI dataset represent a solid contribution to the field, and the paper's findings highlight crucial areas for future research. However, the limited scope of LLMs evaluated and the potential for bias in the human evaluation prevent a higher score. The LLM as Judge method also raises serious questions, calling for further study into the proper use of LLMs for evaluation. While the study contains subjectivity in its interpretation of “deeper” adaptation, the analysis contributes insights that should provide useful future guidance.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Interpreting vision transformers via residual replacement model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17401v1)
- **Authors**: Jinyeong Kim, Junhyeok Kim, Yumin Shim, Joohyeok Kim, Sunyoung Jung, Seong Jae Hwang
- **Abstract**: How do vision transformers (ViTs) represent and process the world? This paper addresses this long-standing question through the first systematic analysis of 6.6K features across all layers, extracted via sparse autoencoders, and by introducing the residual replacement model, which replaces ViT computations with interpretable features in the residual stream. Our analysis reveals not only a feature evolution from low-level patterns to high-level semantics, but also how ViTs encode curves and spatial positions through specialized feature types. The residual replacement model scalably produces a faithful yet parsimonious circuit for human-scale interpretability by significantly simplifying the original computations. As a result, this framework enables intuitive understanding of ViT mechanisms. Finally, we demonstrate the utility of our framework in debiasing spurious correlations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel framework for interpreting vision transformers (ViTs) called the "residual replacement model" (RRM). This method systematically analyzes features learned by ViTs, across all layers and different architectures, extracted using sparse autoencoders (SAEs).  The key idea is to replace ViT computations with interpretable features derived from SAEs within the residual stream.  This simplification allows for scalable and faithful circuit identification, providing insights into how ViTs represent and process visual information. The paper includes a large-scale feature analysis with human annotations, identifies specific feature types (curve and position detectors), and demonstrates the utility of the framework by debiasing spurious correlations in ImageNet classifiers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the systematic application of sparse autoencoders to *all* layers of ViTs, coupled with the RRM's focus on the residual stream.  While SAEs have been used for interpretability in language models and, to some extent, in vision, a comprehensive analysis spanning all ViT layers and different architectures is a significant contribution.  The RRM itself is a clever adaptation of the replacement model concept from LMs, but its focus on the residual stream to circumvent attention's complexity *is* a valuable refinement that makes circuit discovery significantly more tractable in the visual domain.  The finding of curve and position detectors, while known in CNNs, is valuable validation and adds to the growing understanding of feature universality in different types of architectures.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a long-standing challenge: understanding the "black box" nature of ViTs. By offering a tractable method for circuit identification and visual feature analysis, the work facilitates deeper understanding of ViT mechanisms.  The demonstration of debiasing spurious correlations is a practical application showcasing the framework's value beyond mere interpretability. The comprehensive dataset of annotated features is also a valuable resource for future research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:**  The large-scale analysis of features across all layers and multiple ViT variants is a major strength, offering a holistic view of ViT representations. The use of human annotations is a solid and reliable way to interpret complex visual models.
    *   **Scalability:** The RRM approach significantly improves the scalability of circuit discovery, making it feasible to analyze complex models with numerous tokens (which CNN explanation approaches often struggle with). The focus on the residual stream as a "communication channel" is well-motivated.
    *   **Practical Application:**  The debiasing experiments provide convincing evidence of the RRM's practical utility in addressing real-world problems and highlights how the findings can be used for actionable interventions in the models.
    *   **Thorough Evaluation:**  The paper includes thorough quantitative evaluations (faithfulness, completeness, causality) and ablations that rigorously validate the RRM's effectiveness.
    *   **Resource Provision:** The code, SAE weights, and annotated datasets all released to support future research and enhance reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Human Annotation:**  The reliance on human annotation, while reliable, makes the process time-consuming and subjective (even with mitigations). While vision models have more difficulty with automated interpretablity methods, an effort to evaluate or find a more robust method could be helpful in the long run. Automated feature interpretation remains a challenge, but it is a direction that will be needed to make progress in the field.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Attention Mechanisms:** While the paper argues that focusing on the residual stream bypasses the complexity of attention, this also means that the *specific functions* of attention mechanisms and feed-forward networks are only *indirectly* captured. A future iteration of RRM can expand on ways that the attention layer is affected.
    *   **Still Limited to Small Images:** It is good that CNN explanation methods are circumvented, but that only leaves us still to wonder if it can be scaled up to larger images and the same human-scale interpretability.
    *   **Complexity of Visual Explanations:** Some visual patterns are innately more challenging to describe succinctly. The use of heatmaps alongside the input is a valuable tool to quickly assess what parts of the model are activated, but still lacks the "easy to understand" simplicity of LMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of ViT interpretability. It provides a solid foundation for future research focused on: (1) developing automated feature interpretation methods, (2) exploring the roles of attention and feed-forward networks more directly within the RRM framework, (3) applying the RRM to a wider range of ViT architectures and tasks, and (4) using the RRM for more sophisticated debugging and model improvement tasks.
*  **Further areas of investigation**: There is also potential to extend this model to more complex models, such as GNNs, which could benefit from this replacement-style model to explain relationships in the layers of the network. Another possibility is the potential to extend this to other architectures such as transformers which are not ViTs.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** This paper makes a substantial contribution to the field of ViT interpretability. It offers a novel and scalable framework (RRM), provides a comprehensive feature analysis, validates its utility through debiasing experiments, and provides resources to the research community. The weaknesses (dependence on human annotation, incomplete treatment of attention) are acknowledged and represent avenues for future work, and do not detract strongly from the value of the contributions. The impact on future research is expected to be considerable.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DINVMark: A Deep Invertible Network for Video Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17416v1)
- **Authors**: Jianbin Ji, Dawen Xu, Li Dong, Lin Yang, Songhan He
- **Abstract**: With the wide spread of video, video watermarking has become increasingly crucial for copyright protection and content authentication. However, video watermarking still faces numerous challenges. For example, existing methods typically have shortcomings in terms of watermarking capacity and robustness, and there is a lack of specialized noise layer for High Efficiency Video Coding(HEVC) compression. To address these issues, this paper introduces a Deep Invertible Network for Video watermarking (DINVMark) and designs a noise layer to simulate HEVC compression. This approach not only in creases watermarking capacity but also enhances robustness. DINVMark employs an Invertible Neural Network (INN), where the encoder and decoder share the same network structure for both watermark embedding and extraction. This shared architecture ensures close coupling between the encoder and decoder, thereby improving the accuracy of the watermark extraction process. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed scheme significantly enhances watermark robustness, preserves video quality, and substantially increases watermark embedding capacity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DINVMark paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DINVMark, a deep invertible network for video watermarking designed to improve watermarking capacity, robustness, and video quality, especially in the context of HEVC compression.  DINVMark employs an Invertible Neural Network (INN) architecture with a shared encoder/decoder for watermark embedding and extraction.  A differentiable distortion layer is designed to simulate HEVC compression artifacts, making the watermark more resilient to these attacks. The experimental results suggest that DINVMark enhances watermark robustness, preserves video quality, and increases embedding capacity compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The use of INNs in video watermarking is not entirely new, as the paper itself acknowledges existing work that uses INNs for image and video watermarking.  However, the specific combination of an INN with a tailored differentiable HEVC compression simulation layer, and its application to video watermarking to enhance both capacity and robustness, represents a genuine, albeit incremental, contribution. The shared encoder/decoder architecture inherent to INNs does offer a theoretical advantage in coupling the embedding and extraction processes.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant problem: protecting video content in the face of widespread compression. While improving robustness to HEVC is important, the significance depends on how much better it performs over other approaches. The experimental results indicate improved robustness and watermark capacity, which are significant. The high accuracy claimed under various attacks, if substantiated, is a valuable advancement.

* **Strengths:**
    * **HEVC Simulation:** The design of a differentiable distortion layer tailored to simulate HEVC compression artifacts is a key strength.  This allows the network to learn how to embed watermarks that are more resistant to this specific type of attack.
    * **Shared Architecture:** Using the INN structure for both encoding and decoding is elegant and promotes a closer coupling between embedding and extraction.  This *could* lead to more accurate watermark recovery.
    * **Robustness Claims:**  The paper claims excellent robustness against several attacks, as demonstrated in the experimental results.
    * **Lightweight Nature**:  According to the paper, the proposed method offers a lightweight nature of the network, enabling efficient implementation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Cropping Attack Vulnerability:**  The paper acknowledges a limitation in robustness against cropping attacks due to the spatial domain embedding. This is a significant drawback in practical scenarios where such attacks are common.  This weakens the overall robustness claims. The paper needs to address this weakness in future work.
    * **Transcoding:** The absence of evaluation under transcoding attacks leaves a gap in assessing its real-world applicability.
    * **Limited Novelty:** The core INN architecture isn't entirely novel to watermarking. The main novel aspect is the HEVC compression layer and the claims of increased capacity.
    * **Experimental Clarity:** The results show impressive performance, especially the very high accuracy rates. However, the specifics of the datasets and attack parameters should be extremely detailed to enable reproducibility and rigorous comparison to other methods. It would be helpful to provide the source code.
    * **Justification of INN Choice:** The paper does not provide a deep, mathematically-driven justification for choosing an INN architecture *specifically* for *video* watermarking. It provides a basic overview of how INNs can perform embedding and extraction but lacks a thorough argument for why INNs are superior to other deep learning architectures in this context, particularly considering the temporal correlation of video data.

* **Potential Influence:**  If the robustness claims hold up under more rigorous scrutiny (especially regarding cropping and transcoding attacks), DINVMark could influence future video watermarking techniques. The HEVC simulation layer could be adopted by other researchers in the field.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents an incremental improvement in video watermarking using a combination of established techniques (INNs) with a novel HEVC simulation layer. The experimental results are promising, but the acknowledged vulnerability to cropping attacks and the lack of evaluation against transcoding significantly diminish its practical value and impact. The paper's claims require more thorough validation and a deeper theoretical justification.

Score: 6

Rigorous Rationale: The score reflects the balance between the incremental novelty of the HEVC simulation layer, the promising but not definitive experimental results, and the significant weaknesses, particularly the cropping attack vulnerability and lack of evaluation of the method under transcoding processes. While the reported high accuracy rates under specific attacks are impressive, the lack of robustness against a common real-world attack limits the paper's overall impact and significance. A deeper theoretical justification for the choice of INNs and a more comprehensive evaluation are needed to justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Evaluating Multimodal Large Language Models with Daily Composite Tasks in Home Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17425v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenliang Zhang, Yuxi Wang, Hongzhao Xie, Shiyun Zhao, Mingyuan Liu, Yujie Lu, Xinyi He, Zhenku Cheng, Yujia Peng
- **Abstract**: A key feature differentiating artificial general intelligence (AGI) from traditional AI is that AGI can perform composite tasks that require a wide range of capabilities. Although embodied agents powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs) offer rich perceptual and interactive capabilities, it remains largely unexplored whether they can solve composite tasks. In the current work, we designed a set of composite tasks inspired by common daily activities observed in early childhood development. Within a dynamic and simulated home environment, these tasks span three core domains: object understanding, spatial intelligence, and social activity. We evaluated 17 leading proprietary and open-source MLLMs on these tasks. The results consistently showed poor performance across all three domains, indicating a substantial gap between current capabilities and general intelligence requirements. Together, our tasks offer a preliminary framework for evaluating the general capabilities of embodied agents, marking an early but significant step toward the development of embodied MLLMs and their real-world deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a benchmark for evaluating the general abilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) in simulated home environments. The benchmark consists of eight composite tasks inspired by common daily activities of young children, covering object understanding, spatial intelligence, and social activity. The authors evaluated 17 leading proprietary and open-source MLLMs on these tasks, finding that they consistently perform poorly, highlighting a significant gap between current MLLM capabilities and the requirements of artificial general intelligence (AGI). The authors argue that existing AI evaluation approaches have limitations when applied to AGI evaluation, and propose their composite tasks as a step towards a more comprehensive evaluation framework for embodied agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Task Design:** The tasks designed are well-motivated, drawing inspiration from child development milestones to create a realistic assessment of an agent's general abilities. The composite nature of the tasks (requiring perception, reasoning, and action) aligns well with the goal of evaluating general intelligence.
    *   **Focus on Embodiment:** The emphasis on embodied interaction within a simulated environment is a significant contribution. This addresses the limitations of purely language-based or vision-based benchmarks, which often fail to capture the complexities of real-world interaction.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Pipeline:** The authors present a clear and well-defined evaluation pipeline, encompassing perception, reasoning, and action. This allows for a systematic analysis of MLLM-based agents in complex, interactive settings.
    *   **Benchmarking a Broad Range of Models:** The authors evaluated a good range of models from both industry and open source, covering a variety of capabilities.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simulation vs. Reality:** The reliance on a simulated environment is a major limitation. While simulations offer control and standardization, they may not accurately reflect the challenges of real-world interaction. Real-world validation is crucial to confirm the findings.
    *   **Limited Scope of Tasks:** Although the chosen tasks are well-motivated, they represent only a small subset of the diverse activities that a truly general intelligence should be able to perform. Expanding the task set to cover a broader range of domains and complexities would strengthen the benchmark. The tasks are too simplistic and don't really capture the nuances of social interaction or the challenges of true object manipulation.
    *   **Lack of Difficulty Standardization:** The tasks were not standardized across difficulty, leading to potential bias in the evaluation. Some tasks may be inherently easier or more difficult than others, which could skew the results. The difficulty level seems to be targeting very young children and thus many higher-level skills aren't even evaluated.
    *   **Limited Complexity:** The task complexity is relatively low, resembling that of very young children's activities. Truly assessing general intelligence requires more intricate tasks that demand advanced problem-solving, planning, and adaptation. The evaluation really just tests "can the model do X?" instead of assessing how creatively or efficiently the models solve the problems.
    *   **Metric limitations:** The scoring methods rely on evaluating final state alone and there may be many paths to achieving the same final state, and thus a more robust evaluation method should also take the execution path into consideration (e.g. how many interactions are needed to achieve a final state?)

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field by emphasizing the importance of embodied interaction and composite tasks in evaluating AGI. While other benchmarks exist, this work stands out for its focus on daily activities inspired by early childhood development and the assessment of general abilities in a simulated home environment. However, the reliance on simulation and the limited scope of tasks somewhat diminish the paper's novelty and significance. The contribution is incremental and the current limitations prevent it from being truly impactful.

**Justification:**

The paper's contribution to the field of AGI evaluation is promising, especially for the focus on embodied interaction. However, its novelty is diminished by the limitations of the simulation, the narrow range of evaluated tasks, and the task simplicity. The results are predictable: existing MLLMs perform poorly on these embodied tasks. While this provides a valuable data point, it doesn't offer groundbreaking insights. The lack of a standardization framework means that some of the insights about relative differences in model performance should be considered cautiously. With further refinement and real-world validation, this benchmark could become a more significant contribution.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### QWHA: Quantization-Aware Walsh-Hadamard Adaptation for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17428v1)
- **Authors**: Hyesung Jeon, Seojune Lee, Beomseok Kang, Yulhwa Kim, Jae-Joon Kim
- **Abstract**: The demand for efficient deployment of large language models (LLMs) has driven interest in quantization, which reduces inference cost, and parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT), which lowers training overhead. This motivated the development of quantization-aware PEFT to produce accurate yet efficient quantized models. In this setting, reducing quantization error prior to fine-tuning is crucial for achieving high model accuracy. However, existing methods that rely on low-rank adaptation suffer from limited representational capacity. Recent Fourier-related transform (FT)-based adapters offer greater representational power than low-rank adapters, but their direct integration into quantized models often results in ineffective error reduction and increased computational overhead. To overcome these limitations, we propose QWHA, a method that integrates FT-based adapters into quantized models by employing the Walsh-Hadamard Transform (WHT) as the transform kernel, together with a novel adapter initialization scheme incorporating adaptive parameter selection and value refinement. We demonstrate that QWHA effectively mitigates quantization errors while facilitating fine-tuning, and that its design substantially reduces computational cost. Experimental results show that QWHA consistently outperforms baselines in low-bit quantization accuracy and achieves significant training speedups over existing FT-based adapters. The code is available at https://github.com/vantaa89/qwha.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper "QWHA: Quantization-Aware Walsh-Hadamard Adaptation for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning on Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

This paper introduces QWHA, a novel quantization-aware parameter-efficient fine-tuning (QA-PEFT) method for large language models (LLMs). QWHA employs a Walsh-Hadamard Transform (WHT)-based adapter and a specialized quantization-aware initialization scheme to reduce quantization errors and improve fine-tuning performance. The core contributions are the adoption of WHT for adapter design (facilitating efficient computation and high-fidelity error reconstruction) and a novel initialization strategy that involves channel-wise parameter allocation based on error magnitude (AdaAlloc) and value refinement.  Experimental results demonstrate that QWHA outperforms existing QA-PEFT baselines, including those based on LoRA and FT-based adapters, in low-bit quantization scenarios, while also achieving faster training speeds compared to other FT-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and technically sound approach to a significant problem in LLM deployment: efficient fine-tuning of quantized models. While the individual components are not entirely revolutionary, their integration and adaptation within the QA-PEFT context contribute meaningfully to the field.

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the specific combination of the following elements:
    *   ***WHT-based Adapters:***  While FT-based adapters are not entirely new, the use of WHT specifically in this context for QA-PEFT is novel, taking advantage of its computational efficiency and good performance for outliers. However, the paper could be strengthened by a more detailed theoretical justification for *why* WHT is superior to other transforms for *this specific task*, beyond simply stating its ability to capture sharp transitions.
    *   ***AdaAlloc and Refinement:*** The adaptive parameter allocation strategy (AdaAlloc) is a valuable contribution.  It intelligently distributes the parameter budget across channels based on quantization error magnitude, which is a clear improvement over random or uniform allocation schemes. The refinement stage is also a good idea.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need for efficient and accurate methods for fine-tuning quantized LLMs. Low-bit quantization is essential for deploying LLMs on resource-constrained devices, and parameter-efficient fine-tuning reduces the training burden. The demonstrated performance improvements over existing methods, especially in the 2-bit setting, are significant and practically relevant. The reported speedups over other FT-based methods are also valuable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and the limitations of existing approaches.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The method is well-defined and technically feasible.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experimental evaluation is thorough, comparing QWHA to a variety of baselines across multiple datasets and model architectures.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of individual components.
    *   **Strong Results:** The results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of QWHA over existing methods, especially in low-bit scenarios.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Theoretical Depth:** While the paper provides empirical evidence for the benefits of WHT, a more in-depth theoretical analysis explaining *why* WHT is particularly well-suited for mitigating quantization errors, especially compared to other FT-based methods, would strengthen the paper. Claims of superior reconstruction fidelity, while substantiated by data, need greater theoretical mooring.
    *   **Limited Scope of Datasets:** Although multiple datasets were used, evaluation on a wider range of datasets, particularly those commonly used for evaluating PEFT methods, would increase the generalizability of the results.
    *   **Ablation depth**: While AdaAlloc vs. other budget selection was shown, the impact of varying 't' values or differing per-channel budget sizes was not explored.
    *  **The paper mentioned constant adapter scaling factor, but did not provide an ablation study**

*   **Potential Influence:**  QWHA has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and accurate QA-PEFT methods for LLMs. The WHT-based adapter and the adaptive initialization scheme could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The increased efficiency could facilitate the deployment of LLMs in resource-limited environments.

**Overall:**
The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of parameter-efficient fine-tuning of quantized LLMs. The approach is technically sound, well-motivated, and supported by strong experimental results. While a deeper theoretical analysis and a broader evaluation would strengthen the paper, the practical significance of the demonstrated performance improvements justifies a positive assessment.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**
The score reflects the paper's solid technical contributions and significant practical relevance, balanced against the need for a stronger theoretical analysis and a broader experimental scope. It's a strong, incremental advancement in a hot area. Further work expanding the theoretical justification and dataset coverage could easily push it to a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MedFact: A Large-scale Chinese Dataset for Evidence-based Medical Fact-checking of LLM Responses
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17436v1)
- **Authors**: Tong Chen, Zimu Wang, Yiyi Miao, Haoran Luo, Yuanfei Sun, Wei Wang, Zhengyong Jiang, Procheta Sen, Jionglong Su
- **Abstract**: Medical fact-checking has become increasingly critical as more individuals seek medical information online. However, existing datasets predominantly focus on human-generated content, leaving the verification of content generated by large language models (LLMs) relatively unexplored. To address this gap, we introduce MedFact, the first evidence-based Chinese medical fact-checking dataset of LLM-generated medical content. It consists of 1,321 questions and 7,409 claims, mirroring the complexities of real-world medical scenarios. We conduct comprehensive experiments in both in-context learning (ICL) and fine-tuning settings, showcasing the capability and challenges of current LLMs on this task, accompanied by an in-depth error analysis to point out key directions for future research. Our dataset is publicly available at https://github.com/AshleyChenNLP/MedFact.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MEDFACT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MEDFACT, the first evidence-based Chinese medical fact-checking dataset focusing on LLM-generated responses. The dataset consists of 1,321 medical questions and 7,409 extracted claims, mirroring real-world medical scenarios.
